<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove Sidorenko's conjecture with a bipartite graph on 35 vertices and 66 edges: its homomorphism density in some finite simple graph is smaller than the conjectured lower bound. The same connected graph also disproves the forcing conjecture: at one fixed density, asymptotically matching the edge and pattern densities does not imply quasirandomness.

- **claim-sidorenko-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $H$ be the incidence graph of the $22$ triples on $13$ points in Table <a href="#tab:complex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:complex">1</a>. This bipartite graph has $35$ vertices and $66$ edges. There is a finite simple undirected graph $G$ with at least one edge such that $$t(H,G)<p(G)^{66}.$$
  - Explanation: The explicitly specified bipartite incidence graph violates the conjectured homomorphism-density lower bound in some finite simple host.
- **claim-amplification-and-injective-deficit** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-amplification)): For the same graph $H$ as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the following hold.* 1. *For every $c>0$, there is a finite simple undirected graph $F$ with $p(F)>0$ such that $$t(H,F)<c\,p(F)^{66}.$$* 2. *There are $p_*\in(0,1)$, $\delta_*>0$, and finite simple undirected graphs $F_s$ of orders $n_s\to\infty$ for which $d_s=\|E(F_s)\|/\binom{n_s}{2}\to p_*$ and, for all sufficiently large $s$, $$\operatorname{Inj}(H,F_s) \le(1-\delta_*)(n_s)_{35}d_s^{66}.$$
  - Explanation: Categorical powers amplify the normalized deficit while density varies. Balanced independent blow-ups instead retain a fixed injective-copy deficit at a positive limiting density.
- **claim-failure-of-forcing** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-cor-forcing)): For the same connected bipartite graph $H$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, there are $p\in(0,1)$ and a nonconstant graphon $W$ such that $$t(K_2,W)=p,\qquad t(H,W)=p^{66}.$$ Consequently, $H$ is not $p$-forcing at this density and hence is not forcing, despite being bipartite and containing a cycle.
  - Explanation: At one density, a nonconstant graphon matches the edge and pattern densities of the constant graphon. The resulting finite graph sequence fails quasirandomness.
- **claim-complex-certificates** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-complex)): The complex has the following properties.* 1. *Every pair in $E$ lies in exactly two faces, $\|E\|=33$, and the face-neighbor graph is connected.* 2. *For each bit position, each of its two classes consists of $11$ faces covering all $13$ points. Each class has an ordering in which every face after the first shares a pair with an earlier face and introduces exactly one new point.* 3. *Every $a_e$ is at least $1/3$, and some $a_e$ is greater than $1/3$.* 4. *Color refinement of $H$, starting with a separate color for each part, eventually gives distinct colors to all vertices within either part.
  - Explanation: The fixed triple system supplies double pair occurrence, connected face adjacency, exposure orders, bit separation, and discrete refinement within both incidence parts.
- **claim-rank-layer-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-layer)): There is an integer $D_0$ such that, for every fixed even $D\ge D_0$, the following assertion holds as $q$ tends to infinity through odd primes. Let $X_i$, $i\in I$, and $Y_j$, $j\in J$, be independent uniform elements of $\mathcal S_D$. For every set $B\subseteq\mathscr C$ and every choice of signs $\xi_{ij}\in\{1,-1\}$ on $B$, $$\mathbb E\prod_{(i,j)\in B}K_{\xi_{ij}}(X_i-Y_j) =\mathbb E_\eta\prod_{j\in J} \prod_{\substack{e=\{i,k\}\subset j\\(i,j),(k,j)\in B}} \bigl(1+c_0\eta_e\xi_{ij}\xi_{kj}\bigr)+o(1), \label{eq:rank-layer}$$ where the $\eta_e$, $e\in E$, are independent uniform signs. The error is uniform over all such $B$ and sign choices. The same assertion holds with $Y_j-X_i$ in place of $X_i-Y_j$.
  - Explanation: After fixing a sufficiently large even dimension, signed half-rank incidence expectations converge to the independent pair-sign model, uniformly over the finite activity and sign choices.
- **claim-coordinate-expansion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-expansion)): Assume Equation <a href="#eq:kernel-zero-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kernel-zero-moments">[eq:kernel-zero-moments]</a> and fix an even dimension as in Lemma <a href="#lem:rank-layer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rank-layer">5</a>. For fixed type laws and activation laws, $$\begin{aligned} t_{I,J}(W_q)&=1+ \sum_{a:I\to\mathcal A\,,\ b:J\to\mathcal B} \left(\prod_{i\in I}\pi_{a(i)}\right) \left(\prod_{j\in J}\nu_{b(j)}\right)C_A(a,b)+o(1). \label{eq:kernel-expansion} \end{aligned}$$ The corresponding formula for $W_q^\top$ has type maps $b:I\to\mathcal B$, $a:J\to\mathcal A$ and the corner factors $A_{a(j)b(i)}$.
  - Explanation: Vanishing first sign moments eliminate mixed empty and nonempty labels. Connected face adjacency leaves the constant term and the sum over labels nonempty on every pair.
- **claim-support-forced-negative-term** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-support)): At the identity type assignments $a(i)=i$, $b(j)=j$, the sum in Equation <a href="#eq:kernel-coefficient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kernel-coefficient">[eq:kernel-coefficient]</a> for the base laws has exactly one nonzero term. Its labels and value are $$\alpha_e=U_e\quad(e\in E),\qquad C_{A^{\mathrm{base}}}(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}) =-(\theta/2)^{66}. \label{eq:kernel-negative-term}$$
  - Explanation: At identity types, support intersections force every pair label to be its four-corner set. The surviving overlap product has a negative sign.
- **claim-color-refinement-pinning** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pinning)): Let $F=(P\sqcup Q,L)$ be a finite bipartite graph for which color refinement, starting with the two part colors, becomes discrete within each part. There are real vertex scores $s_t$, $t\in P\sqcup Q$, and pair scores $s_{ab}$, $(a,b)\in P\times Q$, such that the identity is the unique maximizer of $$\mathcal Q(f)=\sum_{u\in P\sqcup Q}s_{f(u)} +\sum_{\{u,v\}\in L,\ u\in P}s_{f(u)f(v)} \label{eq:kernel-pinning-score}$$ over *all* maps $f$ preserving the two parts. In particular the maximum has a positive gap from every other such map; no injectivity or adjacency-preservation condition is imposed.
  - Explanation: Refinement histograms produce vertex and pair scores. A finite weighted combination of round scores uniquely favors identity among every part-preserving map, including maps with repetitions.
- **claim-symmetric-negative-kernel** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-negative-kernel)): Assuming Lemma <a href="#lem:rank-layer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rank-layer">5</a>, there is a symmetric kernel $U:\Omega\times\Omega\to[0,1]$ on a finite probability space with mean $\mu>0$ such that $$t(H,U)<\mu^{66}. \label{eq:kernel-symmetric-gap}$$
  - Explanation: Dilution makes the forward negative coefficient dominate the opposite orientation. The crossed product is symmetric, and scaling preserves its strict normalized density deficit.
- **claim-finite-host-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-host)): Suppose $U$ is a symmetric $[0,1]$-valued kernel on a finite probability space, $\mu=\mathbb EU>0$, and $$\tau=t(H,U),\qquad \Delta=\mu^{66}-\tau>0.$$ For any integer $$n>\frac{595+66\mu^{66}}{\Delta}, \label{eq:kernel-host-size}$$ there is a finite simple graph $G$ on $n$ vertices with $t(H,G)<p(G)^{66}$.
  - Explanation: Independent label and edge sampling, an explicit collision bound, and convexity establish a finite simple realization preserving the strict density deficit.
- **claim-subspace-and-symmetric-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-symmetric-counts)): For $0\leq k\leq n$, the number of $k$-dimensional subspaces of $\mathbb F_q^n$ is $$\label{eq:transverse-grassmann} \genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{k}_q =\prod_{a=0}^{k-1}\frac{q^n-q^a}{q^k-q^a} =q^{k(n-k)}(1+O(q^{-1})).$$ For a uniform matrix $M\in\mathcal S_n$, with $n\geq 1$, $$\mathbb P(M\text{ is nonsingular})=1-O(q^{-1}),\qquad \mathbb P(\chi(\det M)=\xi)=\frac12+O(q^{-1}) \quad(\xi\in\{+1,-1\}).$$ For $1\leq k\leq n$, the probability of rank $k$ and prescribed discriminant sign is $$\label{eq:transverse-signed-rank} \left(\frac12+O(q^{-1})\right)q^{-b(n-k)}.$$ Finally, for $0\leq u\leq n$, $$\label{eq:transverse-nullity} \mathbb P(\dim\ker M\geq u)=O(q^{-b(u)}).$$
  - Explanation: Ordered-basis counting and symmetric-form restriction arguments give subspace counts, nonsingularity and sign estimates, signed rank masses, and nullity tail bounds.
- **claim-split-form-isotropic-count** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-split)): Let $r\geq1$. A nondegenerate symmetric form on a $2r$-dimensional space over $\mathbb F_q$ has an $r$-dimensional totally isotropic subspace if and only if its determinant character is $c_0=\chi((-1)^r)$. Such a form is a direct orthogonal sum of $r$ hyperbolic planes. The total number of its $r$-dimensional totally isotropic subspaces is $$\label{eq:transverse-isotropic-count} N_r=\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}(1+q^a) =2q^{r(r-1)/2}(1+O(q^{-1})).$$ The count includes both families of maximal isotropic subspaces.
  - Explanation: The determinant character identifies split forms. Counting isotropic lines and reducing by a line gives the full maximal-isotropic-subspace count, including both families.
- **claim-restriction-sign-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-restrictions)): Let $\mathcal U$ be any nonempty family of distinct $r$-subspaces of $\mathbb F_q^D$, with $r\geq1$, and let $Q$ be a uniform symmetric form. For $\xi\in\{+1,-1\}$ put $$f_\xi(Q)=\frac1{\|\mathcal U\|}\sum_{U\in\mathcal U} \mathbf 1_{\{Q\|_U\text{ nonsingular of sign }\xi\}}.$$ Uniformly over the family $\mathcal U$, $$\label{eq:transverse-restriction-error} \mathbb E\left\|f_\xi(Q)-\frac12\right\| =O\left(q^{-1/2}+\|\mathcal U\|^{-1/2}\right).$$ The same bound holds after conditioning $Q$ to be nonsingular, or conditioning both $Q$ and $Q-L$ to be nonsingular, uniformly over every fixed symmetric form $L$.
  - Explanation: For a nonempty family of distinct equal-dimensional subspaces, shared-block conditioning controls covariances and yields mean absolute deviation of restriction-sign frequencies. The stated nonsingularity conditionings retain the bound.
- **claim-complementary-image-projection** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-projection)): Suppose $B\in\mathcal S_{2r}$ is nonsingular and $A\in\mathcal S_{2r}$ has rank $r$. Then $$\label{eq:transverse-projection} \mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(B-A)=r\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad AB^{-1}A=A.$$ If $A$ and $A-B$ have discriminant signs $\xi_1,\xi_2$, respectively, under these conditions, then $$\label{eq:transverse-pair-sign} \chi(\det B)=c_0\xi_1\xi_2.$$
  - Explanation: Complementary half-rank images turn the rank condition into an idempotence identity. A basis adapted to those images gives the determinant-sign relation.
- **claim-local-transverse-approximation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-transverse)): Suppose $r>1$. For each face $j$, let $\mathcal T_j$ be the event that all three differences between its point matrices are nonsingular. Then, uniformly over the activity and sign choices, $$\label{eq:transverse-local-l1} \mathbb E\bigl[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal T_j}\|F_j-G_j\|\bigr]=O(q^{-1/2}).$$ Moreover $0\leq F_j\leq C_D$ on $\mathcal T_j$, for a constant $C_D$ independent of $q$, and $0\leq G_j\leq8$ everywhere.
  - Explanation: Half-rank center parametrization and restriction-sign concentration approximate each transverse face factor by its sign model. Uniform local bounds permit products across faces sharing point matrices.
- **claim-joint-determinant-signs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-joint-signs)): For fixed even $D\geq12$, the law of $(\eta_e^X)_{e\in E}$ converges in total variation to that of independent uniform signs on $\{+1,-1\}^E$.
  - Explanation: A good-minor event and quadratic Gauss-sum bounds control every nonempty subset moment. Finite sign-vector expansion then gives total variation convergence.
- **claim-global-transverse-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-transverse-limit)): For every fixed even $D\geq12$, every activity pattern $B\subseteq\mathscr C$, and every prescribed set of incidence signs, $$\label{eq:transverse-limit} \mathbb E\left[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal T} \prod_{(i,j)\in B}K_{\xi_{ij}}(X_i-Y_j)\right] =\mathbb E_\eta\prod_{j\in J}\prod_{\{i,k\}\subseteq A_j} (1+c_0\eta_{\{i,k\}}\xi_{ij}\xi_{kj})+o(1),$$ where the $\eta_e$ are independent uniform signs. At each fixed $D$, the error is uniform over all activity patterns and incidence signs.
  - Explanation: A deterministic telescoping bound combines local approximations without assuming independent local errors. Joint determinant-sign convergence supplies the independent sign model.
- **claim-lagrangian-counting-formulas** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lagrangian-counts)): In a symplectic $2N$-space, the following formulas hold.* 1. *The number of Lagrangians is $L_N$.* 2. *A fixed isotropic $t$-space is contained in exactly $L_{N-t}$ Lagrangians. Consequently its containment probability and the number of isotropic $t$-spaces are, respectively, $$\vartheta_N(t)=\frac{L_{N-t}}{L_N},\qquad I_N(t)=\frac{L_N}{L_{N-t}}\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{t}_q. \label{eq:orbits-containment}$$* 3. *For a fixed Lagrangian $A$, the number of Lagrangians $B$ with $\dim(A\cap B)=h$ is $\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}$. Thus $$\pi_{N,h}:=\mathbb P\{\dim(A\cap B)=h\} =\frac{\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}}{L_N}. \label{eq:orbits-pair-probability}$$ All ordered pairs with the same intersection dimension belong to one symplectic orbit.* *For fixed $N$, these formulas give $$\begin{aligned} L_N&=(1+O_N(q^{-1}))q^{b(N)}, &\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{t}_q&=(1+O_n(q^{-1}))q^{t(n-t)},\\ \vartheta_N(t)&\asymp_N q^{-Nt+\binom t2}, &I_N(t)&\asymp_N q^{2Nt-(3t^2-t)/2},\\ \pi_{N,h}&\asymp_N q^{-b(h)}. \label{eq:orbits-count-exponents} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Symplectic reduction and graph charts give exact total, containment, and pair-intersection counts. Pair transitivity also makes conditional completion probabilities depend only on intersection dimension.
- **claim-signed-chart-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-chart-comparison)): Let $B\subseteq\mathscr C$ be a set of active incidences, with prescribed signs $\xi_{ij}$. Let $F$ be any nonnegative function of the $35$ Lagrangians indexed by $I\sqcup J$. For independent uniform matrices $X_i,Y_j\in\mathcal S_D$ and independent uniform Lagrangians $L_i,M_j$, $$\begin{aligned} &\mathbb E\left[ F((\Gamma_{X_i})_i,(\Gamma_{Y_j})_j) \prod_{(i,j)\in B}K_{\xi_{ij}}(X_i-Y_j)\right]\notag\\ &\qquad\le \beta_D^{-35}\prod_{(i,j)\in B}\frac{\pi_{D,r}}{p_{\xi_{ij}}} \ \mathbb E\left[F((L_i)_i,(M_j)_j) \prod_{(i,j)\in B}J(L_i,M_j)\right]. \label{eq:orbits-chart-comparison} \end{aligned}$$ The multiplier is bounded in terms of $D$ alone for all sufficiently large $q$, uniformly in the active set and its signs. In particular, this comparison applies with $F$ the indicator that some pair in $E$ has positive intersection dimension, or the indicator of any specified intersection profile.
  - Explanation: Dropping signs gives a pointwise unsigned comparison. Removing chart conditioning enlarges a nonnegative expectation, with a multiplier bounded at fixed dimension.
- **claim-individual-triple-orbit-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-triple-orbits)): For three independent uniform Lagrangians, every feasible dimensional profile has probability $O_D(q^{-C})$. More strongly, there is a constant $\kappa_D>0$, independent of $q$ and of the particular orbit, such that for every occurring ordered symplectic orbit $\mathcal O$ of that profile, $$\kappa_D q^{-C}\le \mathbb P\{(L_1,L_2,L_3)\in\mathcal O\} \le \kappa_D^{-1}q^{-C}. \label{eq:orbits-orbit-mass}$$ The constant can be chosen for all odd primes. Moreover, conditional on any fixed ordered pair having intersection dimension $h$, the probability that an independent third Lagrangian completes a specified dimensional profile is $$O_D(q^{-C+b(h)}). \label{eq:orbits-conditional-profile}$$
  - Explanation: Forced-subspace counting gives a profile upper bound. Splitting pair-intersection blocks, bounding residual form orbits, and retaining exact intersections give a matching lower bound for each individual ordered orbit.
- **claim-planted-subspace-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-planted-subspace-cost)): For three independent uniform $r'$-subspaces of a $d$-space, the event of common intersection zero, opposite pair dimensions $p_i$, and span codimension $v$ has probability $$\label{eq:planted-subspace-cost} O\left(q^{-\delta P-\sum_i p_i^2-vw}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Choosing a prospective span and direct pair-intersection spaces, then imposing containments, bounds the subspace-profile probability. Dropping exactness only enlarges the count.
- **claim-cyclic-difference-image** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-planted-difference-image)): Let $A_1,A_2,A_3$ be arbitrary subspaces of a vector space, put $Q_i=A_j\cap A_k$, and put $V=A_1\cap A_2\cap A_3$. The image of the cyclic difference map on symmetric forms is exactly $$\label{eq:planted-difference-image} \left\{(D_1,D_2,D_3): D_1\|_V+D_2\|_V+D_3\|_V=0\right\}.$$ Its codimension in the product of the three form spaces on the $Q_i$ is $b(\dim V)$. Independent uniform $F_i$ give the uniform law on this image.
  - Explanation: The common-space restriction sum is the only constraint on cyclic difference forms. Compatible prescriptions extend to each original form, proving both the image description and its uniform law.
- **claim-three-active-pointwise-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-planted-gain)): For a local profile $(h_1,h_2,h_3,c)$, we choose one pointwise gain exponent independent of $q$ as follows. Let $\mathcal U$ be the union, over all odd prime fields and all ordered orbits of this profile, of the parameter tuples arising from actual quadruples $(L_1,L_2,L_3,Y)$ with the stated point profile and $\dim(L_i\cap Y)=r$, using Equations <a href="#eq:planted-reduced-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:planted-reduced-parameters">[eq:planted-reduced-parameters]</a> and <a href="#eq:planted-subspace-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:planted-subspace-parameters">[eq:planted-subspace-parameters]</a>. Their integer entries are bounded at fixed $D$, so $\mathcal U$ is finite. If $\mathcal U$ is empty, the face factor is zero over every odd prime field. Otherwise the face factor with three active incidences is $O(q^g)$ uniformly over all triples in the profile, where $$\label{eq:planted-gain} g=\max_{\text{tuple}\in\mathcal U}\left\{ g_C+\sum_i b(s_i)-\delta P-\sum_i p_i^2-vw -\sum_i b(t_i)+b(v)\right\},$$ so the selected exponent is independent of $q$.* *Every tuple in $\mathcal U$ satisfies the following necessary constraints: $$\label{eq:planted-feasibility} \begin{gathered} 0\le u\le\min(c,r),\quad 0\le r'=r-u\le d, \quad \|\delta\|\le c,\quad d-\delta=2r',\\ P+T=\sum_i s_i\le d,\qquad 0\le t_i\le p_i+\delta,\qquad 0\le v\le p_i+\delta\quad(1\le i\le3),\qquad w\ge0. \end{gathered}$$ In particular $T\le P+3\delta$ and $d+3\delta\ge0$. These conditions are not claimed sufficient for realizability.
  - Explanation: The planted numerator is divided by individual orbit mass. Maximizing over the finite union of actual geometric tuples yields a field-independent pointwise exponent; the displayed necessary inequalities do not characterize realizability.
- **claim-two-active-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-two-active-gain)): For two active incidences whose point Lagrangians meet in dimension $h$, a valid gain exponent is $$\label{eq:planted-two-active-gain} g_2(h) =\max_{\substack{p+t=h\,;\ 0\le t\le p\le r}} \{b(h)-p^2-b(t)\} =b(\lfloor h/2\rfloor).$$ For zero or one active incidence, the face factor is exactly one.
  - Explanation: The pair planted law separates the intersection inside the center from lift-form nullity. Its finite integer maximization gives the stated gain; fewer active incidences have unit factor.
- **claim-global-profile-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-global-cost)): For every prescribed profile, its probability is $O_D(q^{-\mathcal C})$, where $$\label{eq:global-cost} \mathcal C=\frac12\left( \sum_{j\in J}C_j-\sum_{e\in E}(1-a_e)b(h_e)\right).$$
  - Explanation: Expose fresh points within each bit class using conditional triple-profile bounds. Combining class bounds requires event containment, rather than independence among classes.
- **claim-lower-activity-slack** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-lower-active)): Let $h=\max_{e\subset j}h_e$. If $j$ has at most two active incidences, then $$\label{eq:tail-lower-active} S_j\ge0, \qquad S_j\ge\frac{h^2}{24}-\frac h{12}.$$ Equality $S_j=0$ requires $c_j=0$ and either all three pair dimensions zero or exactly one pair dimension equal to $2$. The latter case requires exactly two active incidences, at that pair’s endpoints.
  - Explanation: The baseline dominates the two-active gain and supplies a quadratic slack bound. Equality restricts common and pair dimensions and the active endpoints.
- **claim-feasible-quadratic-coercivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coercivity)): Every tuple satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:tail-feasible" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tail-feasible">[eq:tail-feasible]</a> satisfies $$\label{eq:coercivity} Q\ge\frac1{342}(P^2+T^2+\delta^2).$$
  - Explanation: Scalar reduction, completing squares, and endpoint estimates establish coercivity on the necessary feasible region. The estimate controls selected reduced dimensions rather than asserting positive definiteness in every variable.
- **claim-residual-profile-reduction** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-residue)): There is an even-dimensional threshold such that, for every even $D$ above it, every feasible profile and every activity set for which $\sum_jg_j\ge\mathcal C$ has the following properties: $$\label{eq:tail-residual-profile} c_j=0\quad(j\in J),\qquad h_e\in\{0,2\}\quad(e\in E), \qquad\sum_jg_j\le\frac23\sum_{e\in E}b(h_e).$$
  - Explanation: A dimension-independent budget bounds pair dimensions by propagation through face adjacency and excess separation. Choosing the ambient dimension afterward leaves only the stated integer equality profiles.
- **claim-strict-endpoint-span** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-span)): Let $H_e$ be subspaces assigned to distinct pairs $e$ of a simple graph. Set $$k=\dim\sum_e H_e,\qquad k_i=\dim\sum_{e\ni i}H_e.$$ Then $\sum_i k_i\ge2k$. If the nonzero spaces do not form a direct sum, then $\sum_i k_i\ge2k+1$.
  - Explanation: Local span increments dominate global increments at both endpoints. A minimal nondirect subfamily forces a strict extra increment at an endpoint in a simple graph.
- **claim-residual-weighted-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-residual-counts)): For a profile satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:tail-residual-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tail-residual-profile">[eq:tail-residual-profile]</a>, let $m$ be the number of pairs with $h_e=2$. The contribution of configurations whose pair spaces are not direct is $O_D(q^{-D+1641})$. The contribution of configurations whose pair spaces are direct is $$O_D\left(q^{-\frac13\sum_e b(h_e)}\right).$$ Here “contribution” includes the product of the center factors. The numerical remainder $1641$ is independent of $D$.
  - Explanation: Nondirect pair spaces incur a dimension-dependent containment saving. Direct pair spaces instead yield independent orthogonality equations whose cross terms cancel against endpoint containment factors.
- **claim-weighted-singular-tail-vanishes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-singular-tail)): For every sufficiently large even $D=2r$ and every activity set $B\subseteq\mathscr C$, $$\label{eq:tail-vanishing} \mathbb E_{L,Y}\left[ \mathbf 1_{\{\exists e=\{i,k\}\in E:\,\dim(L_i\cap L_k)>0\}} \prod_{(i,j)\in B}J(L_i,Y_j)\right]=o(1)$$ as $q\to\infty$ through odd primes, with $D$ fixed. The dimension threshold can be chosen for all activity sets simultaneously.
  - Explanation: After fixing an even dimension above both required thresholds, every singular profile has strict decay from either the first cost or the residual counts. A finite sum proves uniform vanishing over activity choices.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-complex",
        "exact_quote": "The complex has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Every pair in $E$ lies in exactly two faces, $|E|=33$, and the face-neighbor graph is connected.*\n\n2.  *For each bit position, each of its two classes consists of $11$ faces covering all $13$ points. Each class has an ordering in which every face after the first shares a pair with an earlier face and introduces exactly one new point.*\n\n3.  *Every $a_e$ is at least $1/3$, and some $a_e$ is greater than $1/3$.*\n\n4.  *Color refinement of $H$, starting with a separate color for each part, eventually gives distinct colors to all vertices within either part.",
        "source_id": "PM-161.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There is an integer $D_0$ such that, for every fixed even $D\\ge D_0$, the following assertion holds as $q$ tends to infinity through odd primes.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-pinning",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F=(P\\sqcup Q,L)$ be a finite bipartite graph for which color refinement, starting with the two part colors, becomes discrete within each part. There are real vertex scores $s_t$, $t\\in P\\sqcup Q$, and pair scores $s_{ab}$, $(a,b)\\in P\\times Q$, such that the identity is the unique maximizer of $$\\mathcal Q(f)=\\sum_{u\\in P\\sqcup Q}s_{f(u)}\n              +\\sum_{\\{u,v\\}\\in L,\\ u\\in P}s_{f(u)f(v)}\n \\label{eq:kernel-pinning-score}$$ over *all* maps $f$ preserving the two parts. In particular the maximum has a positive gap from every other such map; no injectivity or adjacency-preservation condition is imposed.",
        "source_id": "PM-161.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-symmetric-counts",
        "exact_quote": "For $0\\leq k\\leq n$, the number of $k$-dimensional subspaces of $\\mathbb F_q^n$ is $$\\label{eq:transverse-grassmann}\n \\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{k}_q\n =\\prod_{a=0}^{k-1}\\frac{q^n-q^a}{q^k-q^a}\n =q^{k(n-k)}(1+O(q^{-1})).$$ For a uniform matrix $M\\in\\mathcal S_n$, with $n\\geq 1$, $$\\mathbb P(M\\text{ is nonsingular})=1-O(q^{-1}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb P(\\chi(\\det M)=\\xi)=\\frac12+O(q^{-1})\n \\quad(\\xi\\in\\{+1,-1\\}).$$ For $1\\leq k\\leq n$, the probability of rank $k$ and prescribed discriminant sign is $$\\label{eq:transverse-signed-rank}\n \\left(\\frac12+O(q^{-1})\\right)q^{-b(n-k)}.$$ Finally, for $0\\leq u\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:transverse-nullity}\n \\mathbb P(\\dim\\ker M\\geq u)=O(q^{-b(u)}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-161.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-symmetric-counts",
          "exact_quote": "For $0\\leq k\\leq n$, the number of $k$-dimensional subspaces of $\\mathbb F_q^n$ is $$\\label{eq:transverse-grassmann}\n \\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{k}_q\n =\\prod_{a=0}^{k-1}\\frac{q^n-q^a}{q^k-q^a}\n =q^{k(n-k)}(1+O(q^{-1})).$$ For a uniform matrix $M\\in\\mathcal S_n$, with $n\\geq 1$, $$\\mathbb P(M\\text{ is nonsingular})=1-O(q^{-1}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb P(\\chi(\\det M)=\\xi)=\\frac12+O(q^{-1})\n \\quad(\\xi\\in\\{+1,-1\\}).$$ For $1\\leq k\\leq n$, the probability of rank $k$ and prescribed discriminant sign is $$\\label{eq:transverse-signed-rank}\n \\left(\\frac12+O(q^{-1})\\right)q^{-b(n-k)}.$$ Finally, for $0\\leq u\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:transverse-nullity}\n \\mathbb P(\\dim\\ker M\\geq u)=O(q^{-b(u)}).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-161.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $0\\leq k\\leq n$, the number of $k$-dimensional subspaces of $\\mathbb F_q^n$ is $$\\label{eq:transverse-grassmann}\n \\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{k}_q\n =\\prod_{a=0}^{k-1}\\frac{q^n-q^a}{q^k-q^a}\n =q^{k(n-k)}(1+O(q^{-1})).$$ For a uniform matrix $M\\in\\mathcal S_n$, with $n\\geq 1$, $$\\mathbb P(M\\text{ is nonsingular})=1-O(q^{-1}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb P(\\chi(\\det M)=\\xi)=\\frac12+O(q^{-1})\n \\quad(\\xi\\in\\{+1,-1\\}).$$ For $1\\leq k\\leq n$, the probability of rank $k$ and prescribed discriminant sign is $$\\label{eq:transverse-signed-rank}\n \\left(\\frac12+O(q^{-1})\\right)q^{-b(n-k)}.$$ Finally, for $0\\leq u\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:transverse-nullity}\n \\mathbb P(\\dim\\ker M\\geq u)=O(q^{-b(u)}).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-split",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r\\geq1$. A nondegenerate symmetric form on a $2r$-dimensional space over $\\mathbb F_q$ has an $r$-dimensional totally isotropic subspace if and only if its determinant character is $c_0=\\chi((-1)^r)$. Such a form is a direct orthogonal sum of $r$ hyperbolic planes. The total number of its $r$-dimensional totally isotropic subspaces is $$\\label{eq:transverse-isotropic-count}\n N_r=\\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}(1+q^a)\n =2q^{r(r-1)/2}(1+O(q^{-1})).$$ The count includes both families of maximal isotropic subspaces.",
        "source_id": "PM-161.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-split",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r\\geq1$.           "source_id": "PM-161.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $r\\geq1$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-transverse-restrictions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal U$ be any nonempty family of distinct $r$-subspaces of $\\mathbb F_q^D$, with $r\\geq1$, and let $Q$ be a uniform symmetric form.         "exact_quote": "Suppose $r>1$. For each face $j$, let $\\mathcal T_j$ be the event that all three differences between its point matrices are nonsingular.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lagrangian-counts",
        "exact_quote": "In a symplectic $2N$-space, the following formulas hold.*\n\n1.  *The number of Lagrangians is $L_N$.*\n\n\n2.  *A fixed isotropic $t$-space is contained in exactly $L_{N-t}$ Lagrangians. Consequently its containment probability and the number of isotropic $t$-spaces are, respectively, $$\\vartheta_N(t)=\\frac{L_{N-t}}{L_N},\\qquad\n     I_N(t)=\\frac{L_N}{L_{N-t}}\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{t}_q.\n     \\label{eq:orbits-containment}$$*\n\n\n3.  *For a fixed Lagrangian $A$, the number of Lagrangians $B$ with $\\dim(A\\cap B)=h$ is $\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}$. Thus $$\\pi_{N,h}:=\\mathbb P\\{\\dim(A\\cap B)=h\\}\n     =\\frac{\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}}{L_N}.\n     \\label{eq:orbits-pair-probability}$$ All ordered pairs with the same intersection dimension belong to one symplectic orbit.*\n\n*For fixed $N$, these formulas give $$\\begin{aligned}\n L_N&=(1+O_N(q^{-1}))q^{b(N)},\n &\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{t}_q&=(1+O_n(q^{-1}))q^{t(n-t)},\\\\\n \\vartheta_N(t)&\\asymp_N q^{-Nt+\\binom t2},\n &I_N(t)&\\asymp_N q^{2Nt-(3t^2-t)/2},\\\\\n \\pi_{N,h}&\\asymp_N q^{-b(h)}.\n \\label{eq:orbits-count-exponents}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-161.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lagrangian-counts",
          "exact_quote": "In a symplectic $2N$-space, the following formulas hold.*\n\n1.  *The number of Lagrangians is $L_N$.*\n\n\n2.  *A fixed isotropic $t$-space is contained in exactly $L_{N-t}$ Lagrangians. Consequently its containment probability and the number of isotropic $t$-spaces are, respectively, $$\\vartheta_N(t)=\\frac{L_{N-t}}{L_N},\\qquad\n     I_N(t)=\\frac{L_N}{L_{N-t}}\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{t}_q.\n     \\label{eq:orbits-containment}$$*\n\n\n3.  *For a fixed Lagrangian $A$, the number of Lagrangians $B$ with $\\dim(A\\cap B)=h$ is $\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}$. Thus $$\\pi_{N,h}:=\\mathbb P\\{\\dim(A\\cap B)=h\\}\n     =\\frac{\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}}{L_N}.\n     \\label{eq:orbits-pair-probability}$$ All ordered pairs with the same intersection dimension belong to one symplectic orbit.*\n\n*For fixed $N$, these formulas give $$\\begin{aligned}\n L_N&=(1+O_N(q^{-1}))q^{b(N)},\n &\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{t}_q&=(1+O_n(q^{-1}))q^{t(n-t)},\\\\\n \\vartheta_N(t)&\\asymp_N q^{-Nt+\\binom t2},\n &I_N(t)&\\asymp_N q^{2Nt-(3t^2-t)/2},\\\\\n \\pi_{N,h}&\\asymp_N q^{-b(h)}.\n \\label{eq:orbits-count-exponents}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-161.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In a symplectic $2N$-space, the following formulas hold.*\n\n1.  *The number of Lagrangians is $L_N$.*\n\n\n2.  *A fixed isotropic $t$-space is contained in exactly $L_{N-t}$ Lagrangians. Consequently its containment probability and the number of isotropic $t$-spaces are, respectively, $$\\vartheta_N(t)=\\frac{L_{N-t}}{L_N},\\qquad\n     I_N(t)=\\frac{L_N}{L_{N-t}}\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{t}_q.\n     \\label{eq:orbits-containment}$$*\n\n\n3.  *For a fixed Lagrangian $A$, the number of Lagrangians $B$ with $\\dim(A\\cap B)=h$ is $\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}$. Thus $$\\pi_{N,h}:=\\mathbb P\\{\\dim(A\\cap B)=h\\}\n     =\\frac{\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}}{L_N}.\n     \\label{eq:orbits-pair-probability}$$ All ordered pairs with the same intersection dimension belong to one symplectic orbit.*\n\n*For fixed $N$, these formulas give $$\\begin{aligned}\n L_N&=(1+O_N(q^{-1}))q^{b(N)},\n &\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{t}_q&=(1+O_n(q^{-1}))q^{t(n-t)},\\\\\n \\vartheta_N(t)&\\asymp_N q^{-Nt+\\binom t2},\n &I_N(t)&\\asymp_N q^{2Nt-(3t^2-t)/2},\\\\\n \\pi_{N,h}&\\asymp_N q^{-b(h)}.\n \\label{eq:orbits-count-exponents}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
      "explanation": "Symplectic reduction and graph charts give exact total, containment, and pair-intersection counts. Pair transitivity also makes conditional completion probabilities depend only on intersection dimension.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-chart-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subseteq\\mathscr C$ be a set of active incidences, with prescribed signs $\\xi_{ij}$. Let $F$ be any nonnegative function of the $35$ Lagrangians indexed by $I\\sqcup J$. For independent uniform matrices $X_i,Y_j\\in\\mathcal S_D$ and independent uniform Lagrangians $L_i,M_j$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\mathbb E\\left[\n F((\\Gamma_{X_i})_i,(\\Gamma_{Y_j})_j)\n \\prod_{(i,j)\\in B}K_{\\xi_{ij}}(X_i-Y_j)\\right]\\notag\\\\\n &\\qquad\\le\n \\beta_D^{-35}\\prod_{(i,j)\\in B}\\frac{\\pi_{D,r}}{p_{\\xi_{ij}}}\n \\ \\mathbb E\\left[F((L_i)_i,(M_j)_j)\n \\prod_{(i,j)\\in B}J(L_i,M_j)\\right].\n \\label{eq:orbits-chart-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The multiplier is bounded in terms of $D$ alone for all sufficiently large $q$, uniformly in the active set and its signs. In particular, this comparison applies with $F$ the indicator that some pair in $E$ has positive intersection dimension, or the indicator of any specified intersection profile.",
        "source_id": "PM-161.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-chart-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subseteq\\mathscr C$ be a set of active incidences, with prescribed signs $\\xi_{ij}$. Let $F$ be any nonnegative function of the $35$ Lagrangians indexed by $I\\sqcup J$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-triple-orbits",
        "exact_quote": "For three independent uniform Lagrangians, every feasible dimensional profile has probability $O_D(q^{-C})$. More strongly, there is a constant $\\kappa_D>0$, independent of $q$ and of the particular orbit, such that for every occurring ordered symplectic orbit $\\mathcal O$ of that profile, $$\\kappa_D q^{-C}\\le\n \\mathbb P\\{(L_1,L_2,L_3)\\in\\mathcal O\\}\n \\le \\kappa_D^{-1}q^{-C}.\n \\label{eq:orbits-orbit-mass}$$ The constant can be chosen for all odd primes. Moreover, conditional on any fixed ordered pair having intersection dimension $h$, the probability that an independent third Lagrangian completes a specified dimensional profile is $$O_D(q^{-C+b(h)}).\n \\label{eq:orbits-conditional-profile}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-161.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-planted-gain",
        "exact_quote": "For a local profile $(h_1,h_2,h_3,c)$, we choose one pointwise gain exponent independent of $q$ as follows. Let $\\mathcal U$ be the union, over all odd prime fields and all ordered orbits of this profile, of the parameter tuples arising from actual quadruples $(L_1,L_2,L_3,Y)$ with the stated point profile and $\\dim(L_i\\cap Y)=r$, using Equations <a href=\"#eq:planted-reduced-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:planted-reduced-parameters\">[eq:planted-reduced-parameters]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:planted-subspace-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:planted-subspace-parameters\">[eq:planted-subspace-parameters]</a>. Their integer entries are bounded at fixed $D$, so $\\mathcal U$ is finite. If $\\mathcal U$ is empty, the face factor is zero over every odd prime field. Otherwise the face factor with three active incidences is $O(q^g)$ uniformly over all triples in the profile, where $$\\label{eq:planted-gain}\n g=\\max_{\\text{tuple}\\in\\mathcal U}\\left\\{\n g_C+\\sum_i b(s_i)-\\delta P-\\sum_i p_i^2-vw\n                   -\\sum_i b(t_i)+b(v)\\right\\},$$ so the selected exponent is independent of $q$.*\n\n\n*Every tuple in $\\mathcal U$ satisfies the following necessary constraints: $$\\label{eq:planted-feasibility}\n \\begin{gathered}\n 0\\le u\\le\\min(c,r),\\quad 0\\le r'=r-u\\le d,\n \\quad |\\delta|\\le c,\\quad d-\\delta=2r',\\\\\n P+T=\\sum_i s_i\\le d,\\qquad\n 0\\le t_i\\le p_i+\\delta,\\qquad\n 0\\le v\\le p_i+\\delta\\quad(1\\le i\\le3),\\qquad w\\ge0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ In particular $T\\le P+3\\delta$ and $d+3\\delta\\ge0$. These conditions are not claimed sufficient for realizability.",
        "source_id": "PM-161.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "exact_statement": "For a local profile $(h_1,h_2,h_3,c)$, we choose one pointwise gain exponent independent of $q$ as follows. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **homomorphism density and edge density** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For finite simple undirected graphs $H$ and $G$ with $\|V(G)\|\ge1$, write $$t(H,G)=\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{hom}}(H,G)}{\|V(G)\|^{\|V(H)\|}}, \qquad p(G)=\frac{2\|E(G)\|}{\|V(G)\|^2},$$ where $\mathop{\mathrm{hom}}(H,G)$ counts every edge-preserving map, including noninjective maps.
- **incidence graph and corners** ([source](#sec:complex)): The graph used throughout the paper is the incidence graph $$H=(I\sqcup J,\mathscr C),\qquad \mathscr C=\{(i,j):i\in j\}.$$
- **color-refinement update** ([source](#sec:complex)): A refinement step replaces a vertex’s color by the ordered pair consisting of its old color and the histogram of its neighbors’ old colors.
- **positive-rank symmetric-matrix sign** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For a symmetric matrix of positive rank, its *sign* is the quadratic character of the determinant of its induced nondegenerate symmetric form on the quotient by the radical.
- **signed normalized rank layer** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Fix a positive even dimension $D=2r$ and set $$K_\xi(Z)= \frac{\mathbf 1_{\{\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}Z=r,\ \operatorname{sign}(Z)=\xi\}}} {\mathbb P_{Z'\in\mathcal S_D}(\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}Z'=r,\ \operatorname{sign}(Z')=\xi)}, \qquad \xi\in\{1,-1\},\qquad c_0=\chi((-1)^r). \label{eq:kernel-rank-definition}$$
- **oriented bipartite kernel density** ([source](#eq:kernel-bipartite-density)): For a kernel $W:\mathcal X\times\mathcal Y\to[0,\infty)$ on two finite probability spaces, write $$t_{I,J}(W)=\mathbb E\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathscr C}W(x_i,y_j), \qquad W^\top(y,x)=W(x,y), \label{eq:kernel-bipartite-density}$$ where all $x_i\in\mathcal X$ and $y_j\in\mathcal Y$ are sampled independently.
- **totally isotropic subspace for a symmetric form** ([source](#pm-h-16)): A subspace is *totally isotropic* for a symmetric bilinear form if the form vanishes on every pair of vectors in that subspace.
- **Lagrangian subspace** ([source](#pm-h-21)): A subspace is *isotropic* if $\omega$ vanishes on it; a *Lagrangian* is an isotropic subspace of dimension $N$.
- **unsigned normalized incidence kernel** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Define the unsigned normalized incidence kernel by $$J(L,M)=\frac{\mathbf 1_{\{\dim(L\cap M)=r\}}}{\pi_{D,r}}. \label{eq:orbits-unsigned-kernel}$$
- **feasible dimensional profile** ([source](#eq:orbits-profile-cost)): We call a dimensional profile *feasible* if it is realized by an ordered triple of Lagrangians over the field under consideration.
- **planted law** ([source](#pm-h-29)): Define the *planted law* $\nu$ by first choosing $Y$ uniformly and then, conditional on $Y$, choosing the three $L_i$ independently and uniformly subject to $\dim(L_i\cap Y)=r$.
- **fixed-density forcing** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A graph $F$ with $m$ edges is *$p$-forcing* if every sequence of finite simple graphs $G_n$ with $\|V(G_n)\|\to\infty$, $p(G_n)\to p$, and $t(F,G_n)\to p^m$ is quasirandom of density $p$: for every fixed finite simple graph $J$, one has $t(J,G_n)\to p^{\|E(J)\|}$.

| q-incidence-vertices | vertex count of the fixed incidence graph | = | $35$ | Exact fixed combinatorial count. |

| q-pair-separation | bit-separation fraction on a point pair | ≥ | $1/3$ | Uniform over point pairs; strict separation occurs on some pair. |
| q-negative-identity-coefficient | base-law coefficient at the identity type assignment | = | $-(\theta/2)^{66}$ | The activation parameter is fixed, and exactly one corner sign is negative. |
| q-finite-host-size | sufficient finite host order | > | $\frac{595+66\mu^{66}}{\Delta}$ | The positive mean and strict deficit are fixed before selecting host order. |
| q-host-collision-error | absolute difference between expected sampled pattern density and kernel density | ≤ | $\frac{595}{n}$ | All homomorphisms are counted; the collision estimate holds at finite host order. |
| q-signed-rank-mass | mass of a prescribed signed half-rank layer | = | $\left(\frac12+O(q^{-1})\right)q^{-b(r)}$ | Even matrix dimension is fixed before the field size tends to infinity. |
| q-split-isotropic-count | number of maximal totally isotropic subspaces of a split symmetric form | = | $\prod_{a=0}^{r-1}(1+q^a)$ | The count includes both maximal-isotropic families. |
| q-restriction-sign-deviation | mean absolute deviation of the restriction-sign frequency from balance | = | $O\left(q^{-1/2}+\|\mathcal U\|^{-1/2}\right)$ | Uniform in the family; the stated nonsingularity conditionings are allowed. |
| q-transverse-local-error | transverse local mean absolute approximation error | = | $O(q^{-1/2})$ | Dimension is fixed; activity and sign choices are uniform. |
| q-local-sign-model-bound | local sign-model factor | ≤ | $8$ | The model bound holds everywhere, including outside the transverse event. |
| q-joint-sign-dimension-condition | even dimension sufficient for the joint-sign lemma | ≥ | $12$ | This condition concerns joint signs and the transverse branch, not the entire singular-tail argument. |
| q-total-lagrangians | number of Lagrangians in a symplectic space | = | $\prod_{j=1}^N(q^j+1)$ | Exact product including the empty-dimension convention. |
| q-orbit-mass-lower | probability of an occurring ordered triple orbit | ≥ | $\kappa_D q^{-C}$ | Fixed ambient dimension; uniform over occurring orbits and odd primes. |
| q-orbit-mass-upper | probability of an occurring ordered triple orbit | ≤ | $\kappa_D^{-1}q^{-C}$ | Fixed dimension; uniform over the field and actual orbit. |
| q-two-active-exponent | valid two-active gain exponent | = | $b(\lfloor h/2\rfloor)$ | Intersection dimension is fixed; the finite integer maximum is attained by realizable parameters. |
| q-coercivity-bound | homogeneous quadratic slack | ≥ | $\frac1{342}(P^2+T^2+\delta^2)$ | The coefficient is uniform in ambient dimension. |
| q-nondirect-weighted-residual | weighted contribution of nondirect residual pair spaces | = | $O_D(q^{-D+1641})$ | The numerical exponent remainder is independent of dimension; the contribution includes center factors. |
| q-direct-weighted-residual | weighted contribution of direct residual pair spaces | = | $O_D\left(q^{-\frac13\sum_e b(h_e)}\right)$ | The contribution includes all center factors; singular residual profiles give strict decay. |

          "exact_quote": "For every injective map $f:V(H)\\to\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}$, its image vertices have independent labels, and its $66$ source edges use distinct unordered host pairs. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-complex-certificates** [claim](#stmt-prop-complex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-complex): Verify reciprocal pair occurrences and exposure orders, compare bit labels, and recover point refinement from incidence refinement. -> Connected double-occurrence complex with exposure certificates, separation, and discrete part refinement.
- **step-symmetric-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-symmetric-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-symmetric-counts): Count ordered bases; use Schur complements, image parametrization, and radical containment. -> Subspace, signed rank, nonsingularity, and nullity estimates.
- **step-split-isotropic-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-transverse-split) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-split): Split hyperbolic planes and count line-subspace incidences recursively. -> Split-sign criterion and count of both maximal-isotropic families.
- **step-restriction-concentration** [claim](#stmt-lem-transverse-restrictions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-restrictions): Condition on common restriction blocks, bound covariance, and apply the mean absolute deviation estimate under high-probability conditioning. -> Uniform restriction-sign concentration.
- **step-complementary-projection** [claim](#stmt-lem-transverse-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-projection): Use complementary images to identify a projection and take determinant characters in an adapted basis. -> Rank-projection equivalence and pair-sign compatibility.
- **step-local-transverse** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-transverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-transverse): Parametrize centers by isotropic or arbitrary half-dimensional subspaces, normalize layer counts, and average restriction-sign errors. -> Local mean absolute approximation and bounded transverse center factors.
- **step-joint-signs** [claim](#stmt-lem-joint-signs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-joint-signs): Establish good inverse-minor ranks before conditioning; average a remaining row vector by quadratic Gauss sums and recover the sign law from subset moments. -> Joint determinant-sign convergence in total variation.
- **step-transverse-assembly** [claim](#stmt-prop-transverse-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transverse-limit): Integrate independent centers, telescope bounded local products, remove the exceptional-event indicator from the bounded model, and use joint-sign convergence. -> Global transverse rank-layer limit.
- **step-lagrangian-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-lagrangian-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lagrangian-counts): Use symplectic containment quotients, line counting, and symmetric graph charts; establish pair transitivity. -> Exact Lagrangian counts and fixed-dimension estimates.
- **step-chart-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-chart-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chart-comparison): Drop discriminant signs and remove simultaneous chart conditioning using nonnegativity. -> Bounded comparison from signed matrix expectations to unsigned Lagrangian expectations.
- **step-individual-orbit-mass** [claim](#stmt-prop-triple-orbits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-triple-orbits): Count forced common and pair spaces; split symplectic blocks; bound residual form orbits; impose separate avoidance events and exact quotient intersections. -> Two-sided individual orbit mass and conditional profile completion bounds.
- **step-planted-subspace-cost** [claim](#stmt-lem-planted-subspace-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-planted-subspace-cost): Choose a prospective span and direct pair spaces, impose separate containments, and discard exactness only to enlarge counts. -> Subspace-profile cost exponent.
- **step-cyclic-difference-image** [claim](#stmt-lem-planted-difference-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-planted-difference-image): Extend compatible common-space and pair-space form prescriptions, then use uniformity of a linear image. -> Exact cyclic-difference image, codimension, and uniform distribution.
- **step-three-active-gain** [claim](#stmt-prop-planted-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-planted-gain): Represent the center factor as an orbit density ratio; count center-common intersections, reduced subspaces, and lift nullities; maximize over actual tuples. -> A field-independent pointwise gain and necessary feasibility inequalities.
- **step-two-active-gain** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-active-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-active-gain): Divide pair planted probabilities by ambient pair mass and evaluate the decreasing finite integer maximand. -> The two-active gain and unit factors for fewer active incidences.
- **step-global-profile-cost** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-cost): Expose a fresh point along each bit-class tree and combine containing class events without an independence assumption. -> Global point-profile probability cost.
- **step-lower-activity-slack** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail-lower-active) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-lower-active): Expand the baseline, compare it with the two-active exponent, and determine integer equality cases. -> Nonnegative lower-activity slack with a quadratic bound and equality restrictions.
- **step-quadratic-coercivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-coercivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coercivity): Complete squares, reduce to scalar sums, minimize over the allowed interval endpoints, and treat both signs of the shift. -> A dimension-uniform coercive lower bound.
- **step-residual-profiles** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-residue): Bound pair dimensions uniformly by a global budget and connectivity propagation; choose dimension afterward and classify integer zero-slack profiles. -> Residual profiles with zero common dimensions and restricted pair dimensions.
- **step-strict-span** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail-span) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-span): Compare global and endpoint-local span increments, then use an inclusion-minimal nondirect family. -> Endpoint span inequality with a strict nondirect increment.
- **step-residual-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail-residual-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-residual-counts): For nondirect families count arbitrary ambient spans and apply the strict endpoint increment; for direct families count independent orthogonality equations and cancel containment terms. -> Strict weighted savings for direct and nondirect residual configurations at the required dimension.
- **step-singular-tail** [claim](#stmt-prop-singular-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-singular-tail): Fix dimension above the residue threshold and the nondirect bound; sum first-cost and residual savings over the finite profile family. -> Vanishing weighted singular contribution, uniformly over activity sets.
- **step-rank-layer-completion** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-layer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-singular-tail-2): Combine the transverse limit with chart comparison and the unsigned singular-tail bound; use finite-choice uniformity and negation symmetry. -> The complete signed rank-layer limit in both orientations.
- **step-coordinate-expansion** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-expansion): Factor independent coordinates, expand parity products, average activation moments, and propagate nonempty pair labels through connected faces. -> Oriented density expansion over all type maps, including repeated types.
- **step-support-forcing** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-support): Prove the support-intersection identity, force every nonempty pair label, and multiply the signed overlap moments. -> A unique negative identity-type coefficient.
- **step-type-pinning** [claim](#stmt-lem-pinning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pinning): Build round scores from squared histogram differences and combine them with dominating finite weights. -> Vertex and pair scores uniquely maximizing identity over all part-preserving maps.
- **step-negative-symmetric-kernel** [claim](#stmt-prop-negative-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-negative-kernel): Use score-weighted priors and thinning to dominate competitors, dilute inactive types to control orientation, take the crossed product, and scale. -> A finite symmetric bounded kernel with positive mean and strict density deficit.
- **step-finite-host** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-host) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-host): Sample independent labels and loopless unordered edges; bound source collisions and apply convexity to obtain a strict expected deficit. -> Existence of a finite simple host at any integer order above the stated bound.
- **step-main-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-singular-tail-2): Apply finite-host transfer to the constructed negative symmetric kernel. -> The finite simple Sidorenko counterexample.
- **step-amplification** [claim](#stmt-cor-amplification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-singular-tail-3): Factor homomorphisms through categorical powers; separately use balanced independent blow-ups and collision control for injective maps. -> Arbitrarily small normalized homomorphism ratio and a fixed-density injective deficit sequence.
- **step-forcing-failure** [claim](#stmt-cor-forcing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-singular-tail-4): Interpolate equal-mean independent-coordinate kernels between opposite density inequalities; prove nonconstancy by orthogonality and sample a sequence with excess four-cycle density. -> An exact graphon equality at one density and a nonquasirandom finite sequence.

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-transverse",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $r>1$. For each face $j$, let $\\mathcal T_j$ be the event that all three differences between its point matrices are nonsingular. Then, uniformly over the activity and sign choices, $$\\label{eq:transverse-local-l1}\n \\mathbb E\\bigl[\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal T_j}|F_j-G_j|\\bigr]=O(q^{-1/2}).$$ Moreover $0\\leq F_j\\leq C_D$ on $\\mathcal T_j$, for a constant $C_D$ independent of $q$, and $0\\leq G_j\\leq8$ everywhere.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-lagrangian-counts",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lagrangian-counts",
          "exact_quote": "In a symplectic $2N$-space, the following formulas hold.*\n\n1.  *The number of Lagrangians is $L_N$.*\n\n\n2.  *A fixed isotropic $t$-space is contained in exactly $L_{N-t}$ Lagrangians. Consequently its containment probability and the number of isotropic $t$-spaces are, respectively, $$\\vartheta_N(t)=\\frac{L_{N-t}}{L_N},\\qquad\n     I_N(t)=\\frac{L_N}{L_{N-t}}\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{t}_q.\n     \\label{eq:orbits-containment}$$*\n\n\n3.  *For a fixed Lagrangian $A$, the number of Lagrangians $B$ with $\\dim(A\\cap B)=h$ is $\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}$. Thus $$\\pi_{N,h}:=\\mathbb P\\{\\dim(A\\cap B)=h\\}\n     =\\frac{\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{N}{h}_q q^{b(N-h)}}{L_N}.\n     \\label{eq:orbits-pair-probability}$$ All ordered pairs with the same intersection dimension belong to one symplectic orbit.*\n\n*For fixed $N$, these formulas give $$\\begin{aligned}\n L_N&=(1+O_N(q^{-1}))q^{b(N)},\n &\\genfrac{[}{]}{0pt}{}{n}{t}_q&=(1+O_n(q^{-1}))q^{t(n-t)},\\\\\n \\vartheta_N(t)&\\asymp_N q^{-Nt+\\binom t2},\n &I_N(t)&\\asymp_N q^{2Nt-(3t^2-t)/2},\\\\\n \\pi_{N,h}&\\asymp_N q^{-b(h)}.\n \\label{eq:orbits-count-exponents}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-161.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-lagrangian-counts",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite nondegenerate symplectic spaces."
      ],
      "method": "Use symplectic containment quotients, line counting, and symmetric graph charts; establish pair transitivity.",
      "output": "Exact Lagrangian counts and fixed-dimension estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-lagrangian-counts"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-chart-comparison",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-chart-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subseteq\\mathscr C$ be a set of active incidences, with prescribed signs $\\xi_{ij}$. Let $F$ be any nonnegative function of the $35$ Lagrangians indexed by $I\\sqcup J$. For independent uniform matrices $X_i,Y_j\\in\\mathcal S_D$ and independent uniform Lagrangians $L_i,M_j$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\mathbb E\\left[\n F((\\Gamma_{X_i})_i,(\\Gamma_{Y_j})_j)\n \\prod_{(i,j)\\in B}K_{\\xi_{ij}}(X_i-Y_j)\\right]\\notag\\\\\n &\\qquad\\le\n \\beta_D^{-35}\\prod_{(i,j)\\in B}\\frac{\\pi_{D,r}}{p_{\\xi_{ij}}}\n \\ \\mathbb E\\left[F((L_i)_i,(M_j)_j)\n \\prod_{(i,j)\\in B}J(L_i,M_j)\\right].\n \\label{eq:orbits-chart-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The multiplier is bounded in terms of $D$ alone for all sufficiently large $q$, uniformly in the active set and its signs. In particular, this comparison applies with $F$ the indicator that some pair in $E$ has positive intersection dimension, or the indicator of any specified intersection profile.",
          "source_id": "PM-161.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-chart-comparison",
      "inputs": [
        "Signed matrix layer masses.",

      ],
      "method": "Drop discriminant signs and remove simultaneous chart conditioning using nonnegativity.",
      "output": "Bounded comparison from signed matrix expectations to unsigned Lagrangian expectations.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-chart-comparison"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-triple-orbits",
          "exact_quote": "For three independent uniform Lagrangians, every feasible dimensional profile has probability $O_D(q^{-C})$. More strongly, there is a constant $\\kappa_D>0$, independent of $q$ and of the particular orbit, such that for every occurring ordered symplectic orbit $\\mathcal O$ of that profile, $$\\kappa_D q^{-C}\\le\n \\mathbb P\\{(L_1,L_2,L_3)\\in\\mathcal O\\}\n \\le \\kappa_D^{-1}q^{-C}.\n \\label{eq:orbits-orbit-mass}$$ The constant can be chosen for all odd primes. Moreover, conditional on any fixed ordered pair having intersection dimension $h$, the probability that an independent third Lagrangian completes a specified dimensional profile is $$O_D(q^{-C+b(h)}).\n \\label{eq:orbits-conditional-profile}$$",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-planted-gain",
          "exact_quote": "For a local profile $(h_1,h_2,h_3,c)$, we choose one pointwise gain exponent independent of $q$ as follows. Let $\\mathcal U$ be the union, over all odd prime fields and all ordered orbits of this profile, of the parameter tuples arising from actual quadruples $(L_1,L_2,L_3,Y)$ with the stated point profile and $\\dim(L_i\\cap Y)=r$, using Equations <a href=\"#eq:planted-reduced-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:planted-reduced-parameters\">[eq:planted-reduced-parameters]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:planted-subspace-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:planted-subspace-parameters\">[eq:planted-subspace-parameters]</a>. Their integer entries are bounded at fixed $D$, so $\\mathcal U$ is finite. If $\\mathcal U$ is empty, the face factor is zero over every odd prime field. Otherwise the face factor with three active incidences is $O(q^g)$ uniformly over all triples in the profile, where $$\\label{eq:planted-gain}\n g=\\max_{\\text{tuple}\\in\\mathcal U}\\left\\{\n g_C+\\sum_i b(s_i)-\\delta P-\\sum_i p_i^2-vw\n                   -\\sum_i b(t_i)+b(v)\\right\\},$$ so the selected exponent is independent of $q$.*\n\n\n*Every tuple in $\\mathcal U$ satisfies the following necessary constraints: $$\\label{eq:planted-feasibility}\n \\begin{gathered}\n 0\\le u\\le\\min(c,r),\\quad 0\\le r'=r-u\\le d,\n \\quad |\\delta|\\le c,\\quad d-\\delta=2r',\\\\\n P+T=\\sum_i s_i\\le d,\\qquad\n 0\\le t_i\\le p_i+\\delta,\\qquad\n 0\\le v\\le p_i+\\delta\\quad(1\\le i\\le3),\\qquad w\\ge0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ In particular $T\\le P+3\\delta$ and $d+3\\delta\\ge0$. These conditions are not claimed sufficient for realizability.",
          "source_id": "PM-161.1"
        }

      "output": "A field-independent pointwise gain and necessary feasibility inequalities.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-planted-gain"

          "exact_quote": "There is an integer $D_0$ such that, for every fixed even $D\\ge D_0$, the following assertion holds as $q$ tends to infinity through odd primes. For every set $B\\subseteq\\mathscr C$ and every choice of signs $\\xi_{ij}\\in\\{1,-1\\}$ on $B$, $$\\mathbb E\\prod_{(i,j)\\in B}K_{\\xi_{ij}}(X_i-Y_j)\n =\\mathbb E_\\eta\\prod_{j\\in J}\n \\prod_{\\substack{e=\\{i,k\\}\\subset j\\\\(i,j),(k,j)\\in B}}\n \\bigl(1+c_0\\eta_e\\xi_{ij}\\xi_{kj}\\bigr)+o(1),\n \\label{eq:rank-layer}$$ where the $\\eta_e$, $e\\in E$, are independent uniform signs. The error is uniform over all such $B$ and sign choices. The same assertion holds with $Y_j-X_i$ in place of $X_i-Y_j$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-expansion",
          "exact_quote": "Assume Equation <a href=\"#eq:kernel-zero-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:kernel-zero-moments\">[eq:kernel-zero-moments]</a> and fix an even dimension as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:rank-layer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:rank-layer\">5</a>. For fixed type laws and activation laws, $$\\begin{aligned}\n t_{I,J}(W_q)&=1+\n \\sum_{a:I\\to\\mathcal A\\,,\\ b:J\\to\\mathcal B}\n \\left(\\prod_{i\\in I}\\pi_{a(i)}\\right)\n \\left(\\prod_{j\\in J}\\nu_{b(j)}\\right)C_A(a,b)+o(1).\n \\label{eq:kernel-expansion}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The corresponding formula for $W_q^\\top$ has type maps $b:I\\to\\mathcal B$, $a:J\\to\\mathcal A$ and the corner factors $A_{a(j)b(i)}$.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-two-active-integer-maximum**: For the admissible intersection dimensions, the finite integer maximum defining the two-active gain exponent equals the triangular number of half the intersection dimension rounded down. This exponent bounds the center factor asymptotically; it is not the center factor itself.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-exponent-as-center-integral**: Replace the actual two-incidence center integral by the computed integer gain exponent. -> The computed value is an exponent for an asymptotic upper bound, not an exact integral or probability.
- **anti-use-three-active-substitution**: Apply the two-active formula to a face with three active incidences. -> The three-active estimate depends on the common intersection, reduced-center subspaces and lift forms, which the scalar calculation omits.
- **anti-use-necessary-constraints-as-realizability**: Treat every tuple satisfying the displayed dimension inequalities as a realizable configuration. -> The inequalities are necessary and do not certify the existence of the underlying subspaces.
- **anti-use-scalar-tests-as-counterexample-certificate**: Treat successful scalar maximization tests as verification of the full Sidorenko counterexample. -> These tests do not establish the rank-layer limit, the weighted singular estimate, the negative kernel coefficient or a finite host witness.
- **anti-use-transverse-threshold-for-whole-proof**: Use the transverse dimension threshold as a sufficient threshold for the entire rank-layer argument. -> The singular contribution requires a further sufficiently large fixed dimension.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The finite integer maximization in the two-active gain formula is directly executable. The source proves that its maximum occurs at the smallest admissible integer parameter and evaluates it by parity. This harness checks that arithmetic identity on explicit inputs; it does not certify the asymptotic geometric bound or the main existence theorem.

```python
# INPUTS: A JSON object containing integer r and h.
# PRECONDITIONS: r >= 1 and 0 <= h <= 2*r; Boolean values are excluded.
# OUTPUTS: The integer two-active gain exponent, followed by a newline.
# TIME: Finite integer evaluation; no source complexity bound is asserted.
# SPACE: Finite integer storage; no source complexity bound is asserted.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision integer arithmetic using fmpz.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces a nonzero exit.
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpz


def triangular(n):
    return n * (n + 1) // 2


def main():
    data = json.load(sys.stdin)
    if not isinstance(data, dict) or set(data) != {'r', 'h'}:
        raise ValueError('invalid input object')
    r_raw, h_raw = data['r'], data['h']
    if type(r_raw) is not int or type(h_raw) is not int:
        raise ValueError('integer parameters required')
    r, h = fmpz(r_raw), fmpz(h_raw)
    if r < 1 or h < 0 or h > 2 * r:
        raise ValueError('inadmissible intersection dimension')
    value = triangular(h // 2)
    print(int(value))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-specified-pattern** (formal_scope): The counterexample concerns the specified bipartite incidence graph. It does not invalidate the inequality for every bipartite pattern or for the positive graph families discussed in the paper. Blocked use: ['Rejecting an established positive result for a different pattern solely because this counterexample exists.']
- **limit-all-homomorphisms** (formal_scope): The main density comparison counts all edge-preserving maps, including maps with repeated images. Injective maps and induced copies have different counting contracts. Blocked use: ['Replacing the main homomorphism density by an injective or induced-copy density without a separate transfer argument.']
- **limit-fixed-parameters-before-field-limit** (hypothesis): The field size tends to infinity through odd primes only after the dimension and every finite construction parameter are fixed. Dimension-dependent asymptotic constants are allowed. Blocked use: ['Taking a simultaneous growing-dimension and growing-field limit using the fixed-dimension estimates.', 'Applying the signed symmetric-form arguments in even characteristic.']
- **limit-full-dimension-threshold** (hypothesis): The transverse dimension condition does not suffice for the whole argument. The singular contribution also requires the residue threshold and the nondirect residual saving; the displayed nondirect dimension bound is not asserted to be a threshold for the whole proof. Blocked use: ['Treating the transverse dimension condition as a complete parameter certificate for the counterexample.', 'Treating the nondirect residual bound alone as the complete rank-layer threshold.']
- **limit-weighted-singular-events** (approximation): Small unweighted singular-event probability does not control products of normalized rank indicators. The weighted singular contribution needs its own estimate. Blocked use: ['Discarding singular configurations solely because their probability tends to zero.']
- **limit-zero-moment-activation-laws** (hypothesis): The coordinate expansion requires vanishing nonempty first sign moments and fixed type and activation laws. Arbitrary activation distributions do not inherit the same all-nonempty coefficient expansion. Blocked use: ['Changing branch probabilities or sign biases without checking every required first moment.', 'Applying the density expansion while the type or activation laws vary with the field size.']
- **limit-identity-coefficient-not-averaged-density** (formal_scope): The unique negative support-forced term is established at identity type assignments for the prescribed base laws. The actual density averages every type assignment, so negativity at identity alone does not establish the final deficit. Blocked use: ['Skipping the comparison with competing type assignments after checking the identity coefficient.', 'Transferring the support-forcing conclusion to altered activation supports.']
- **limit-discrete-refinement-pinning** (hypothesis): Pinning requires color refinement from the two part colors to become discrete within each part. The competing maps preserve parts but need not be injective or preserve adjacency. The resulting gap is a score gap. Blocked use: ['Claiming unique identity pinning for a graph whose refinement stabilizes with indistinguishable vertices.', 'Checking the objective only on injective homomorphisms.', 'Interpreting the combinatorial score gap as a spectral gap.']
- **limit-ordered-parameter-selection** (nonconstructive): The negative-kernel argument selects scores and thinning, then dilution, and then a sufficiently large odd prime. The existence proof does not supply a numerical kernel or a computed field-size threshold for its final strict gap. Blocked use: ['Claiming an explicit counterexample kernel merely from the parameter-selection argument.', 'Increasing the field size before fixing the dilution and other finite parameters.']
- **limit-finite-host-transfer-input** (hypothesis): Finite-host transfer assumes a symmetric probability-valued kernel on a finite probability space, a positive mean, and a strictly positive density gap. Its sufficient host-order bound depends on that supplied gap. Blocked use: ['Applying the transfer to an asymmetric rectangular kernel before symmetrization.', 'Using an unscaled kernel value as an edge probability.', 'Using the host-order bound when the density gap is zero or unproved.']
- **limit-host-existence-not-every-sample** (nonconstructive): The expectation and convexity argument guarantees some successful simple-graph realization. It does not guarantee a deficit for every sample or identify a successful adjacency relation. Blocked use: ['Reporting an unchecked sampled host as a certified counterexample.', 'Interpreting the sufficient host order as a deterministic success guarantee for one random draw.']
- **limit-sampling-edge-independence** (hypothesis): Labels are sampled independently, and unordered edges between distinct host vertices are sampled independently conditional on labels. A noninjective source map may reuse a host edge, so its repeated edge occurrences use the same Bernoulli trial. Blocked use: ['Drawing independent edge trials for repeated occurrences of the same host edge in a source map.', 'Introducing loops into the finite simple sampling construction.', 'Assuming independence for colliding source maps.']
- **limit-restriction-sign-family** (hypothesis): Restriction-sign concentration assumes a nonempty family of distinct subspaces of the same positive dimension. Its stated conditioning allowances concern nonsingularity, not arbitrary subsequent sign or profile conditioning. Blocked use: ['Applying the family-size error improvement to a multiset that repeats one subspace.', 'Applying the bound to an empty family or mixed subspace dimensions.', 'Assuming the same concentration bound after conditioning on sign matches.']
- **limit-transverse-local-approximation** (approximation): The local mean absolute approximation and the bounded center-factor estimate apply on transverse point configurations and require the stated half-dimension condition. They are not pointwise equality statements and do not replace singular-tail analysis. Blocked use: ['Replacing every center factor by its sign model exactly.', 'Extending the transverse center-factor bound to singular configurations.', 'Using the local approximation at the excluded smallest half-dimension.']
- **limit-chart-comparison-nonnegativity** (hypothesis): Removing chart conditioning enlarges the expectation because the test function and normalized kernels are nonnegative. The comparison is an inequality with a bounded dimension-dependent multiplier, not an equality of the signed and unsigned laws. Blocked use: ['Removing the chart event from an expectation with a signed test function.', 'Treating unsigned Lagrangian sampling as exactly the signed matrix-layer distribution.']
- **limit-individual-orbit-denominator** (formal_scope): A pointwise planted center-factor bound uses the mass of each individual ordered symplectic orbit. A whole-profile probability gives only an average of orbit density ratios and cannot replace that denominator. Blocked use: ['Dividing a planted profile probability by an independent profile probability and calling the quotient a pointwise center bound.']
- **limit-actual-tuples-and-conditioning** (hypothesis): The three-active gain is maximized over a finite union of actual geometric tuples across odd prime fields and ordered orbits. The displayed dimension inequalities are necessary but not sufficient for realizability. Product laws are used before imposing the mutual point profile. Blocked use: ['Replacing actual geometric realizability by feasibility of the displayed inequalities alone.', 'Asserting independence of reduced subspaces or lift forms after conditioning on their mutual profile.']
- **limit-two-active-exponent-scope** (formal_scope): The finite integer maximum gives a valid gain exponent for two active incidences. It does not give the exact center integral or the gain for three active incidences. Arithmetic tests of this maximum cover a component of the argument. Blocked use: ['Replacing a center integral by its gain exponent.', 'Applying the two-active formula to a fully active face.', 'Treating finite arithmetic agreement as verification of the rank-layer limit or the main counterexample.']
- **limit-profile-cost-and-local-slack** (formal_scope): The bit-class probability bounds use containment of the complete profile event in each class event, not independence of overlapping classes. Local slack can be negative; the global budget, connectivity, and excess separation control it. Blocked use: ['Multiplying overlapping class-event probabilities as if the events were independent.', 'Assuming every fully active face has nonnegative slack.', 'Using the global profile cost as an equality for the actual profile probability.']
- **limit-residual-counting-prerequisites** (hypothesis): The residual weighted counts apply after the sufficiently large dimension reduction to the stated residual profiles. The strict endpoint-span argument requires distinct pairs in a simple graph. Only endpoint-local spans must be isotropic; the total ambient span need not be. Blocked use: ['Applying residual exponents to arbitrary intersection profiles before the residue reduction.', 'Applying the strict endpoint-span increment to repeated parallel pairs.', 'Restricting the nondirect ambient-span count to isotropic total spans without justification.']
- **limit-amplification-density-scopes** (counterexample_scope): Categorical powers make the normalized homomorphism ratio arbitrarily small while host density varies. Balanced independent blow-ups instead preserve a positive limiting density and produce an eventual fixed injective deficit. Blocked use: ['Claiming arbitrary normalized amplification at a fixed host density.', 'Claiming the injective deficit holds at every finite blow-up order.', 'Treating the injective comparison as an induced-copy statement.']
- **limit-forcing-one-density-asymptotic-sequence** (counterexample_scope): The forcing consequence supplies an exact graphon equality at one density. Its finite graph sequence matches edge and pattern densities only in the limit, and its excess four-cycle density witnesses failure of quasirandomness. Blocked use: ['Claiming failure of fixed-density forcing at every density.', 'Claiming exact edge and pattern equality for each finite sampled graph.', 'Using this example to invalidate the classical edge-and-four-cycle characterization.']

Otherwise the face factor with three active incidences is $O(q^g)$ uniformly over all triples in the profile, where $$\\label{eq:planted-gain}\n g=\\max_{\\text{tuple}\\in\\mathcal U}\\left\\{\n g_C+\\sum_i b(s_i)-\\delta P-\\sum_i p_i^2-vw\n                   -\\sum_i b(t_i)+b(v)\\right\\},$$ so the selected exponent is independent of $q$.*\n\n\n*Every tuple in $\\mathcal U$ satisfies the following necessary constraints: $$\\label{eq:planted-feasibility}\n \\begin{gathered}\n 0\\le u\\le\\min(c,r),\\quad 0\\le r'=r-u\\le d,\n \\quad |\\delta|\\le c,\\quad d-\\delta=2r',\\\\\n P+T=\\sum_i s_i\\le d,\\qquad\n 0\\le t_i\\le p_i+\\delta,\\qquad\n 0\\le v\\le p_i+\\delta\\quad(1\\le i\\le3),\\qquad w\\ge0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ In particular $T\\le P+3\\delta$ and $d+3\\delta\\ge0$. These conditions are not claimed sufficient for realizability.",
          "source_id": "PM-161.1"
        }
      ],

> ## Scope
>
> Sidorenko's conjecture predicts that every bipartite graph $H$ has homomorphism density at least the host graph's edge density raised to $|E(H)|$. The formalization disproves this for the paper's fixed bipartite graph with $35$ vertices and $66$ edges: it constructs a nonempty finite simple host graph with $t(H,G)<t(K_2,G)^{66}$. The separate forcing-conjecture consequence in the paper is outside this statement.

Comparator source `SidorenkoCounterexample.lean` (1764 bytes, sha256 `489688f5c4eb60c6dd8cfe0ef69663aaf11e795d6252e9626972764a50187de9`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! # A finite counterexample to Sidorenko's conjecture

The pattern is the incidence graph of the 22 triples in Table 1 of the source.
Homomorphism density counts all edge-preserving maps, including noninjective maps.
-/

namespace SidorenkoCounterexample

def faces : Fin 22 → Finset (Fin 13) := ![
  {0,1,3}, {0,1,9}, {0,2,3}, {0,2,9}, {1,3,10}, {1,7,10},
  {1,7,12}, {1,9,12}, {2,3,4}, {2,4,9}, {3,4,11}, {3,10,11},
  {4,8,9}, {4,8,11}, {5,6,7}, {5,6,11}, {5,7,10}, {5,10,11},
  {6,7,12}, {6,8,11}, {6,8,12}, {8,9,12}]

abbrev PatternVertex := Fin 13 ⊕ Fin 22

def incidence : PatternVertex → PatternVertex → Prop
  | .inl point, .inr face => point ∈ faces face
  | .inr face, .inl point => point ∈ faces face
  | _, _ => False

def H : SimpleGraph PatternVertex where
  Adj := incidence
  symm.symm := by intro left right; cases left <;> cases right <;> simp [incidence]
  loopless.irrefl := by intro vertex; cases vertex <;> simp [incidence]

noncomputable def homCount {V W : Type*} (F : SimpleGraph V) (G : SimpleGraph W) : ℕ :=
  Nat.card {hom : V → W // ∀ left right, F.Adj left right → G.Adj (hom left) (hom right)}

noncomputable def homDensity {V W : Type*} (F : SimpleGraph V) (G : SimpleGraph W) : ℝ :=
  (homCount F G : ℝ) / (Nat.card W : ℝ) ^ Nat.card V

noncomputable def edgeDensity {W : Type*} (G : SimpleGraph W) : ℝ :=
  2 * (Nat.card G.edgeSet : ℝ) / (Nat.card W : ℝ) ^ 2

/-- A finite simple host with at least one edge violates Sidorenko's inequality. -/
theorem main : ∃ size : ℕ, 0 < size ∧ ∃ host : SimpleGraph (Fin size),
    (∃ left right, host.Adj left right) ∧ homDensity H host < (edgeDensity host) ^ 66 := by
  sorry

end SidorenkoCounterexample

end OAI
```
